# Audit log

How we work, by the owner's word (15 Nov): every push is checked for errors
and no more. That is the test suite once, and one load in a browser when the
change can be seen. Detailed testing is not done per push. It is done in
batches, as an audit, and this file is what an audit works from.

One entry per release to the live branch, newest first. Each says what
changed, what was checked, and **what was not**, which is the list an audit
starts with. When an audit has been through an entry, it says so under it.

| Date | Commit | What changed | Checked | Not checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 19 Nov | claude/suburb-floor (this push, the second commit) | **The Lock-ups' doors open on something** (the owner: "100% of doors are fake each time… a waste"). Most doors now stand 2.4 m out from the wall as a lock-up of their own (block side walls, a lintel to the roof, a concrete floor, a block back wall over the painted sky), the roller at its front, solid while it is shut and not once it is up. What is behind each is rolled (`bays`): **kept things** (steel racking of boxes and tins, a bike against it, a mower; in three of the eight slots an item: battery, snack, torch or bandage), **a car** exactly the size of the lock-up (the estate at half its size, nose to the wall), **somebody living there** (the forest's displaced fox, on a bed of sacking with a tin, breathing; it looks up when the door goes up), or still **the wall** or **the picture of a garage**. Where the room has a way out in one of its lock-up walls (six in ten), a lock-up stands in front of that too with no back, its roller goes up on its own when you come within 2.5 m (and to the remote, and to E), and it is never solid, so it never shuts a doorway. Two lock-ups never share floor (where one along a side wall meets one along the end, the second stays a door in the wall). `roller()` (suburb.js) takes `depth`, `w`, `solid`, `auto`, `onOpen`; the room's old rolls (only `empty`) still build. **What a visitor would notice:** with the remote, doors open onto lock-ups with things in them, a small car, a fox, sometimes an item; a way out of the room may be behind a door that opens as you reach it. | Eight seeds on the dev page at Medium: 46k–78k triangles (was 37k), 11–13 lights, no warnings; all eight doors opened on seed 1 and looked at. 60 suburb floors built in Node with every room's module (`generate`, theme suburb): no doorway walled off; nine of those lock-ups built a way through. Dev seed 5: the way through going up as you come, onto the next room. `npm test` 1543/1543. | Co-op (each door opens for whoever has the remote, as before). The fox at Classic. A creature's path through the way through (it opens only for the visitor). |

| 19 Nov | claude/steam-webgpu (WebGPU: a lost device falls back) | The lead's find: with a software WebGPU adapter that loses its device at the first frame, the picture stayed black. `engine/gpu-recover.js` (new, pure): the order after a loss (a fresh WebGPU device on the same canvas, then WebGL 2 on a canvas of its own, twice) and its bookkeeping. `engine/stage.js`: `openStage` shows one probe frame and waits for any loss; `guardDevice` swaps the renderer's backend in place (same renderer, materials and visit), holds drawing while it recovers (`setDrawing`), counts a loss only from the current device or canvas, and keeps the old canvas on top, unseen, for input and pointer lock. Nothing holds at start: today's renderer. Off by default as before. | `npm test` once, 1540 of 1540 (new `blockrooms-webgpu-device-lost`: a loss leads to a picture, from WebGPU or WebGL 2; throws and refusals passed over; nothing holds → failed, no drawing). | 19 Nov | (fair play: a ticket from the start, and the route checked; the owner, for prizes) | **A visit must have happened to sign the book.** Going in sends the building's seed and gets a ticket the server signs (`visit-ticket.mjs`, HMAC; key `BOOK_TICKET_KEY` or derived from the book's own secret); signing must hand it back: forged, foreign, too fast (claims more time than has passed, 20 s slack), over 14 days old, or already used are refused (`why: 'ticket'`). **The route:** the game records where the visitor was once a second of play (`core/trail.mjs`, `tally.js` `trace`, one optional call in `index.js`); the server rebuilds the building from seed and difficulty and checks parts in order, inside rooms, joined rooms only, no impossible speed, each part left by its way out, samples against seconds (`visit-check.mjs`). A route that does not fit is signed and marked `check: flag: …`, logged, and never shown (`shownEntry`). `api/book.mjs` sets `checks: true`; older tests of other rules pass `checks: false`. Signings may now be 64 KB (the route); anything else stays 4 KB. One signing per ticket replaces one per seed and name. `vercel.json` lists the generator's files for the book function. `docs/BLOCKROOMS-FAIR-PLAY.md`: what is checked, how to read flags, what it cannot catch. | `npm test` once, 1512 of 1512 (new `blockrooms-visit-check`, 6). | 17 Nov | (a shove: the left mouse button, with anything but scissors in the hand) | Early players told the owner there was nothing to fight back with; the owner proposed a weak shove/block on a click and agreed the design. **A click shoves** (scissors in the hand still jab). It hurts nothing and ends nothing. It does two things: whatever is right in front (1.35 m) is pushed back a step if it can be, and **for a moment after, a hit from in front does not land** (0.45 s on Easy and below, 0.32 Normal, 0.22 Hard). It costs 0.3 of a breath (the sprint's breath), there is a second between shoves, no breath comes back for 2.5 s after one, and out of breath there is none: the fourth in a row leaves you winded. Nothing with furniture in the hands. **What each does about a push:** head count is pushed back, turned aside for 1.4 s and goes off the way furthest from you; the intern goes back a step, loses its place and half of what it had learnt; the standing meeting goes over for 1.2 s if it is going about or stood up on end, and takes no notice while charging; Mother's small ones are batted away, out of the air or off the floor, and lie dazed (Mother is not moved); a toner that is coming (dropping, chasing, wound up, in the air) is knocked into its recoil, and one that is only sitting there takes no notice; the mascot, awake and on its feet, slides back a step and nothing else. **Too heavy** (`heavy` in cast.js): the standing meeting and carried interest; a shove does not turn their hit aside. The block is one rule in the conductor (`c.hurt` asks the level before anything else), so it covers every hit of the cast but those two. On a phone the hand button shoves when there is nothing in front of it to use (no new button, so the layout is untouched). The pause menu's list of controls gains one line, Shove: Click. docs/BACKROOMS.md has the contract (`shoveTargets()`, `heavy`, `c.hurt(n, from)`). | `npm test` once on the final tree. New `test/backrooms-shove.test.mjs` (8 tests: breath and cooldown, the window by difficulty, what is in front, what is reached and how many, sliding short of a wall, each creature's rule, that no creature's shove hurts or ends anything, the wiring, and a real player shoving until winded). In a browser on the built site, on Easy: head count's hand took a heart with no shove, none with a shove in time, and one with a shove facing away; a shove moved head count (to its recount) and the intern (1.1 m, blank); the standing meeting went from filing to down; Mother was not moved; a perched toner took no notice. No errors. | Not played by hand: whether it feels good, and whether it is too strong or too weak, is the owner's and the players' to say. Not seen: a toner batted out of a leap, Mother's small ones batted away, the mascot slid back, the other door let go by a block. Not tried on a phone (the hand button). Not tried in co-op: a guest's shove is not sent to the host, so for a guest it probably does nothing. There is no hand to see: the only signs are the camcorder's lurch and a sound, and nothing was heard by me. The toner and the mascot offer themselves to a shove only in the states named above. |

| 16 Nov | (the big creatures made fairer, on what players told the owner) | Players said the new creatures home in with nothing to be done about it; the owner chose four changes. **The standing meeting (the centipede) runs like a bishop:** only ever one of four ways, corner to corner of the building. Stood up on end it takes the diagonal nearest to you and does not change it, so it is only coming for somebody on its diagonal; a step off the line and it goes into the wall. Whatever it hits, a wall or you, it goes down for 2.8 seconds (it used to run on through you to the wall); getting up it finds you from whichever end is nearer, takes a diagonal and comes again. It will not start a run straight into a wall. **Head count is blind and aimless:** it no longer feels footsteps or comes to them; it wanders room to room (a little quicker than before, 1.1 m/s, still about half a walk) and hurts whoever it walks into. **Easy, Training Wheels and Tourist:** head count, the standing meeting and carried interest take one heart, not two, and they, the intern and Mother go 15% slower; Normal and Hard are as before. **None of the four big ones** (head count, standing meeting, intern, carried interest) **is dealt to the floor you land on.** The building's edition moves (to `is8gvd`) because seeds now deal them further down. Wall drawings not touched, as the owner asked. | `npm test` 981 of 981. Rule tests rewritten for both creatures (every direction it ever takes over 2,000 moments is one of the four; it does not re-aim once stood up; down after a wall or a person; re-aims on getting up; head count keeps nothing about where anybody is), a table test, and 400 plans in which none of the four is on the landing floor and each is still met. One browser load on the built site: three charges in a row each measured at exactly 45°, each ended down, each followed by standing up again; head count stayed at its wander with somebody running four metres off. No errors. | Not played by hand: whether the bishop is now too easy or still too hard is the owner's and the players' to say. Not seen: it getting up and leading with its tail end; a run begun from a doorway corner; either creature on Easy against Hard. **Head count's three wall warnings still describe the old one** (feels the floor, stand still, came to where I ran): left alone on the owner's word about drawings, and now untrue. The staff directory on the front page still lacks these four. |
| 16 Nov | (optional accounts and saves that follow you between computers; codex/blockrooms-cloud-saves ccad7ae, joined by the lead) | Codex's one commit, applied to live and joined by hand on the owner's rule: **signed in, the account's name and character win, on every device; not signed in, this browser's own are used as before.** What it brings: an archive page (`/archive`), sign-in by Google or an emailed code through Supabase, and a local archive that every visit is now recorded in, signed in or not (the old local record is imported into it once and left where it was as a backup). The join, in `site.js` and `home.js`: the play page takes the visitor's name and look from the account when there is one and from the studio when there is not; the department kit and the difficulty are this browser's either way, because the archive does not hold them; the studio writes a changed character to the archive, and takes the account's character when signed in; the rule for which visits may sign the book is live's (difficulty), not the branch's older one. **It is switched off unless the hosting build is given the two Supabase settings** (`account-config.json` says `enabled: false` here). No key is in the commit; the build refuses a secret key. | Suite 980 of 980 (its fourteen tests among them). Built site, signed out, as somebody who played before the change (a named custom robot with the courier kit and one kept tape in the old record): the old record imported (name, character, tape, counts) and still there as a backup; the front page shows the name, the tape on the shelf, the way to the archive and the difficulty picker; an ordinary visit took the kit, was given up from its menu, showed its card under the name with the way to the archive on it, and was counted; the archive page loads (200) and says saved on this device. Codex is to try the signed-in path before the settings are given to the live build. |

## Found, not fixed

- **19 Nov, phones: a pass of its own, later (the owner: "we are going to need a whole mobile team to work on optimizing at some point. But not a priority").** Gaps gathered so far, for whoever takes it: no smash button for the extinguisher (right-click only); the extinguisher's held pose, the sticky notes on the lens and the start panel's spinning pass figure never checked on a real phone; the front page's new start panel checked at 390 px in a desktop browser only; no real-phone performance numbers at any graphics tier (the forest and the waterpark's mirror are the heaviest); Safari never run.

- **18 Nov, the cast session: the shadow sometimes has nowhere to stand for a visitor who stands still.** It only takes a place just past one of the room's doorways or in one of its corners, 3–10 m off, out of the picture and in line of sight (`cast/shadow.js` `place`). Measured in node over 40 visits (every plain room, the visitor on a 0.75 m grid, 8 facings, the picture at its widest 16:9; no furniture or stand check, so it errs on the side of a place existing): 18.8% of stances leave it nowhere (forest 25.6%, night 22.0%, mall 20.6%, fitout 14.1%), but only 17 of 4,760 rooms have nowhere from any stance. So it waits only while the visitor holds one stance: any turn or step gives it somewhere, and a visitor standing still in one facing for 30 s is rare. Not fixed (the lead: rare goes here). The dev page's `beside` no longer depends on it (2e1bd92).
- **18 Nov, the gameplay session: the old single stack of paper can land in a doorway's keep-clear.** `inventory.js` `scatter` places what `planSupplies` returns on any open floor cell of its room (and the stash zones of an oddity), with no check of the room's doorways or of a coin's place; on a count of 10 floors with the paper in plenty off, 4 floors had that one stack within a doorway's reach (`0.31 + 0.55 + half the doorway`), one of them near two. The paper in plenty (`plentyOfPaper`) is placed clear of doorways and coins and is not affected. **Not fixed on purpose**: giving the old items a doorway check would move other things' places (they share one random stream). Fix when moving that stream is acceptable: draw each item's cells from its own stream and skip the keep-clear.

- **18 Nov, the forest review: a stepped mound in a doorway's approach, cause not found.** `forest-maze.js` `buildPnwTerrain` is meant to keep clear of every doorway (its apron: the cell loop near "Generous clear apron around every doorway") and of every reserved lane (`c.clear`). On tape 19 (`generate('19', { theme: 'forest' })`), plain room 13 (7.8 × 6.8 m, the `thicket` motif with six baffles, two doorways in its left wall at -1.5 and 2.0 and one in its right), it put a mound 0.65 to 0.97 m high against the left wall from about z = -0.5 back, in a doorway's approach, and the level's check then found that doorway unreachable and switched the room's collision off. It is avoided, not mended: `forest.js` `forestRoom` gives a plain room that has baffles or partitions `hills: false`. To see it again: take that one spread line out of `forestRoom`'s `thicket({ … })` call, build tape 19 whole (the last test in `test/backrooms-forest.test.mjs` does, and fails), and print `world.walkable(rt).floor(x, z)` along x = -3.6 in room 13. Two things worth knowing before starting: `rt.floorY` was not set on that room although its floor was not flat, so the height came through `floorAt(def, …)`; and the two doorways on one wall may be what the apron's test gets wrong. The fifteen deck rooms and open plain rooms keep their mounds and build without the warning on 38 tapes.
- **18 Nov — a harness test that depends on timing.** `test/harness-claude.test.mjs` "Claude Code: the raw frames can be kept for comparison, without the token" asserts that `mcp_set_servers` is sent before `get_usage`; in one full run of the suite they arrived the other way round, and alone the file passes every time. Not the game. Not fixed: the test should not assert the order of two messages the adapter sends independently, or the adapter should send them in a fixed order.
- **17 Nov, the gameplay session, the front page as a phone:** at 390 and 320 wide nothing scrolls sideways, the staff directory's 24 rows fit, the difficulty picker, the high scores (one table with a difficulty filter), the guestbook and the archive link are all there. Two small things at 320 wide only, not mended: the pass kit's label "STD-00 · Standard Issue (0/1 PT)" is cut short with an ellipsis; and the fixed MUSIC: OFF button lies over the right-hand edge of the staff table as the page is scrolled.
- **17 Nov, the gameplay session, the shopping centre's dummies:** the cap of six holds; with six in a room nobody was cut off from a doorway on two seeds. ~~A shoved dummy can come to rest inside another (5 of 29 shoves).~~ Mended by the suburb session the same night (one rule for where a dummy may stand, asked when it is put down and when it is pushed).

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session: the mascot's own first room sometimes has no doorway it fits.** On 3 of 400 floors the room its plinth is in has only crawl holes (it stands 1.62). The room is chosen in gen.js (`pickLair`, about lines 855 to 868: `lairs`, `plainer`, `roomy`), which looks at a room's size, height and depth and never at its doorways; the room is then dressed as its lair (the plinth, the name plate), so it cannot simply be put elsewhere when the floor is built. Not mended, on purpose for now: filtering the pick moves the mascot's room on many seeds to cure three floors in four hundred, and the mascot does get down and reach through a hole. To mend it, the three filters would ask `canLeave(room, 1.62)` (cast/paths.js) and keep the last fallback unfiltered. Recounted after the standing-spot fix of the same night: on 30 office floors (828 rooms) a place that cannot be stood on is handed out in 0 rooms for the mascot and for head count (was 53 and 61) and in 4 for a walker 1.9 m tall (was 57; not looked into: most likely rooms with nowhere at all for one that tall); on 3 waterpark floors, 0 for all three (was 7, 10, 7). The route check between neighbouring rooms on a waterpark floor then gives the same answer with no help: every refusal is a room the walker refuses on purpose.

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session, a first pass over the waterpark (hidden): nothing found wrong, with these gaps.** All ten rooms on three seeds pass the level's own check; every use in them pressed from four sides without error; a visitor thrown at 24 drop edges in each of five pools lands wading or swimming and can walk out (the diving well by its stepped strips at either end, 4 to 6 m along the bottom); a knock during a slide or flume ride does nothing, because a ride freezes the visitor and a frozen visitor is not thrown; a towel cart carried to sixteen spots was set down at eight and refused at eight (over lower floor) and was never lost or put in a wall. Then, on the towel carts: a cart is only ever set down on flat floor (its footprint has to be on one height), so the lead's changed rule fires in the Return Pool only for the basin's flat bottom with the carrier standing in the basin, where picture, collider and height agree (y −0.6, top 0.475); from the deck every spot below was refused. A cart in a doorway's throat shuts it to every walker, the visitor included, until the visitor lifts it again, which they can from either side: a gate, as the room means it to be. Two carts in the two doorways shut the room; no way was found for carts to shut the visitor in for good. **Found, harmless as things are:** while a cart is carried its `y` and `top` keep the height it was last set down at (the basin's), and are put right when it is set down; nothing reads them meanwhile because a carried thing has no collider. Still not tried: any of the cast on a waterpark floor, a creature against a cart in a doorway, a creature shoved into water.

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session: rooms the two widest creatures cannot get into.** Over 280 generated floors (its own seeds, real colliders, 2,679 rooms with a doorway), in 21 rooms (0.8%, about one floor in thirteen) no doorway leaves room for a walker of radius 0.5 (head count), and in 17 (0.6%) none for 0.38 (the standing meeting): a pillar or furniture just outside the throat. By room: plain 6, bus-stop 3, the-culvert 3, strip-mall 3, and one each of garage-row, recess, back-garden, walk-in, the-close, car-park. It measured the floor just outside each doorway, not the doorway, and took no account of door leaves. Such a room is a place those two cannot follow into. Not fixed: where the generator or a room puts things in front of a doorway is what to look at. Also from that pass: on Tourist the shopping centre's food-court hole puts the visitor back at the landing, twice running, as The Gap does; they then walked more slowly than after The Gap's fall (0.5 to 0.7 m in 0.8 s against 1.15), cause not found.

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session: a hit can throw the visitor into The Gap.** Being hit throws them about 0.9 m (player.js `knock`), and the throw looks at no floors: standing 0.5, 0.7, 0.9 and 1.2 m short of the brink and thrown at it, they fell in every time. On every difficulty but Tourist a fall there is the end of the visit, so one hit near the edge is a heart and the visit. The other edges, tried later the same night: **the shopping centre's food-court hole, yes**, from 0.6 m or nearer on the one side its brass rail leaves open (the rail stops a throw as it stops a walk); **the chasm, no**: the room holds the visitor to its balcony, wings and bridges, and 30 throws at their edges put nobody over (the furthest went 0.37 m). No other hole in the shopping centre was found. Whether that is the room's horror or a cheap death is the owner's to say; the guard, if wanted, is in `knock()`: stop short where the floor along the throw drops. Not fixed. From the same pass, and fine: on Tourist a fall into The Gap puts the visitor back at that floor's landing, standing and able to walk, twice running (the path in index.js `env.end` that no test covers); about 600 throws at random spots on four floors put nobody in a wall or off a floor; the sculpted door leaves reach 2 to 3 mm further from the hinge than the old slab, inside the margin a creature waits at. **Decided by the owner, 17 Nov: "no, knockbacks can push the player off edge." It stays as it is: no guard at an edge. Closed.**

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session:** on mall floors (still hidden) the level's own check sometimes warns that a doorway of `the-grotto` "could not be reached — its furniture is no longer solid" (1 of 6 seeds; `seed=qa-mall-the-grotto-1&themes=yellow,mall&depth=1&only=the-grotto&check=1`). The room stays walkable, but something of the grotto's stands in front of a doorway on some layouts and can then be walked through. Passed to the suburb session. From the same pass, about 70 shoves at head count, the intern, the standing meeting and the mascot put nothing through a wall, into a doorway's throat or onto the visitor; the mascot mostly would not be moved (2 of 16).

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session, on its own finding (4) below:** door 4 on seed `qa-door2` is not a faulty doorway. A pillar (0.9 by 0.5 m) stands in front of it and leaves 0.3 m either side of the doorway's middle: walkers of radius 0.2 to 0.4 cross both ways, and one of radius 0.5 cannot, either way. Head count walks at 0.5 (the standing meeting at 0.38), so a room whose only doors are pillared like that is one head count cannot enter. That room has a second door, not tried; how common it is across seeds was not counted. Not fixed: the generator placing a pillar in front of a doorway is the thing to look at.

- **17 Nov, from the gameplay session's QA pass (not fixed, not looked into by the lead):** (1) ~~the-culvert (The Gap): at its default entry on seed `qa-pic` an area of floor is pure black with scattered white tiles and a jagged edge, beside a large crate; it reads as a missing floor.~~ **Meant** (the suburb session, 17 Nov): that is The Gap itself, the strip of lawn the world forgot to draw, a real hole to fall into; the pale squares are pieces of floor falling away, and the crate is a loose hoarding that can be moved. (2) mandatory-fun: the default entry puts the camera among the staff at under half a metre, an arm across the bottom of the picture. (3) keyboard-floor: the screen on the far wall stands proud of the wall it is on, so the camera gets to 0.13 m of it and not the 0.22 m every other wall leaves; nothing is seen through. (4) Doors: on seed `qa-door2`, door 4 lets no walker of radius 0.2 to 0.5 through in either direction, before the door fix and after; cause not found. (5) The "see the other side of a wall" report did not reproduce and could not be ruled out: the session's automatic crack finder gives too many false alarms to say. Measured and fine in the same pass: nothing left on the graphics card after each of 12 whole visits and no growth in memory; the same four seeds built twice are identical; no console errors over 70 floors of every kind of room; every request goes to our own address; the near plane never went through a wall in 72 approaches; no overflow from 320 px to ultrawide.

- ~~The Last Light is in about one visit in a hundred.~~ Put right the same day (see the table): about half of all visits now.

- (Fixed 15 Nov: the Retention Clerk not leaving her place on seed 7. It was a shred bin against her nook, not the furniture.)
- 15 Nov: on seed 12 with `room=shredding-bay`, one of the other residents on the floor has no free floor anywhere round it (seen in passing while checking the clerk). Which one, and whether it matters, not looked at.

## Audits

- **17 Nov, the gameplay session: a whole visit to the way out on each difficulty, against a local score book** (the built site served with its in-memory book; nothing real written; ranked pages, not `?debug`; four parts, about a minute each; the ending by `finish('exit')`, not by leaning on the wall). The card and the book agree on every mode: Training Wheels ×0.5 (1,055 of a base 2,110), Easy ×1 (2,110), Normal ×1.5 (3,165), Hard ×2 (3,670 of a base 1,835: one heart, so "still in one piece" is worth less); each signed, and each appears in its own mode's table and no other, by the API's filter and by the front page's. Tourist and a `?debug` visit show UNSCORED RECORDING and do not sign. A kept tape played back to the way out shows its points again and does not sign again. The book itself refused the session's first, instant visits (under 12 s and two rooms a part) with 422, which is its own check working. No console errors. Not tried: the real way out through the wall, a typed name or message, a strike, a second signing by one browser in quick succession, a visit under real pressure from the cast and the battery.

None yet.
